# jujum-ai audit

Static-read grade 69/100 (at risk): an arithmetic roll-up of model-assigned pass/fail from a source read, not a test result, scan, or certification. Evidence basis: mostly Firm. Biggest risk: AI-generated output is executed with new Function and injected as raw SVG in the renderer. Biggest strength: Timing-safe comparison on every credential check across all three surfaces.

## Scope and method

Commit d826add; fresh audit; web-application form at side-project scale; balanced risk profile. Capabilities: static.
Budget: full. Screening and deep-trace checks are selected for judgment.
Secondary forms: api-service, mobile-desktop.
Context candidates: product/ai-ml-chat (Firm); regulatory/hipaa (Firm, verify).
Assumptions: Deployed and serving real traffic on Render/Vercel free tiers today (render.yaml + deployment docs present; no runtime verification possible in static mode).; Vendored LibreChat/** is third-party upstream code; only its integration and pinning posture are audited.; No regulated data (health/payments/minors) processed by design per PRD v3.1 Section 1.2; HIPAA overlay remains an unverified candidate pending owner confirmation..
The audit made no source edits, ran no application code, connected to no live systems, and called no models unless an explicitly listed capability states otherwise.

Coverage: 312 of 317 applicable checks evaluated (98%). Unknown checks never earn points and cap the verdict.

## Compliance gate

Result: pass. Screened 2026-08-22 with provider-neutral@1. This is a policy-allowability screen, not a legal-compliance determination.

## Depth and escalation

Security and build completeness are deep-capable. Every other domain is screening-grade. A domain label describes this audit method, not the importance of the domain. Scanner and inventory signals remain leads until traced and refuted.

| Domain | Label | Deep-trace state | Escalation criterion | Up to three current finding leads |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| product | screening | none unknown | Escalate when metric definitions cannot be joined to emitted events or user evidence, or when product claims conflict across code and artifacts. | F-PRD-1, F-PRD-2, F-PRD-3 |
| architecture | screening | 1 unknown | Escalate when trust boundaries, load-bearing dependencies, or failure domains cannot be reconstructed without a dedicated architecture review. | F-ARC-1, F-ARC-2, F-ARC-3 |
| stack | screening | none unknown | Escalate when supported-version, compatibility, or migration claims need current ecosystem research or executable upgrade testing. | F-STK-1, F-STK-2, F-STK-3 |
| database | screening | none unknown | Escalate money, tenant isolation, destructive migration, query-plan, and data-integrity leads to a database specialist with runtime metadata access. | F-DB-2, F-DB-1, F-DB-10 |
| security | deep-capable | none unknown | Escalate any Critical or High lead, unresolved authorization boundary, secret exposure, or runtime-only exploit path to an independent security assessment. | F-SEC-1, F-SEC-10, F-SEC-2 |
| llm | screening | none unknown | Escalate privileged model-output sinks, prompt-injection boundaries, agent tools, and quality-critical paths without adversarial eval evidence to an AI security review. | F-LLM-1, F-LLM-2, F-LLM-3 |

## Standards coverage

Control-evidence readiness, not certification. These frameworks evidence the technical controls a code audit can see (encryption, access control, consent code, DSAR paths, audit logging, accessible markup). They do not evidence the organizational and process controls (policies, training, vendor management, incident response, physical security) that SOC 2, ISO/IEC 27001, or PCI certification require. A category below reports control-evidence readiness and never claims certification.

## Strengths

- Timing-safe comparison on every credential check across all three surfaces. E-83 backend/src/server.ts:275: `if (crypto.timingSafeEqual(crypto.createHash("sha256").update(received).digest(), crypto.createHash("sha256").update(expected).digest())) return next();`; E-84 frontend/app/api/auth/route.ts:14: 

```text
function safeEqual(left: string, right: string) {
  const a = crypto.createHash("sha256").update(left).digest();
  const b = crypto.createHash("sha256").update(right).digest();
  return crypto.timingSafeEqual(a, b);}
``` Preserve: Keep hashing-then-timingSafeEqual for every new token or passcode comparison; never introduce plain string equality on secrets.

- Private journal uses layered defense: AES-256-GCM envelope plus HMAC-signed replay-windowed worker auth plus bound D1 queries. E-85 backend/src/lib/private-journal-store.ts:84: 

```text
const signature = crypto.createHmac("sha256", secret).update(`${timestamp}.${bodyText}`).digest("base64url");
```; E-86 cloudflare/journal-store/src/index.ts:42: `Math.abs(Date.now() - seconds * 1000) > 5 * 60 * 1000 || !supplied) return false;` Preserve: Never move the journal encryption key or D1 service secret to the client; keep ciphertext-only storage in D1 and the timestamp replay window on every new worker route.

- Natural-key upserts make retryable writes idempotent end to end. E-87 backend/src/gateway.ts:33: `upsert({where:{sessionId_questionIndex:{sessionId:id.sessionId,questionIndex:id.questionIndex}},update:{skipped:true,...}`; E-88 backend/src/server.ts:2761: 

```text
const expenseId = `bill-${targetBill.id}`;
``` Preserve: Keep client-stable ids (sessionId+questionIndex, date-keyed journals, bill-&#123;id&#125; expenses) so retries converge; apply the same pattern to any new create-or-update flow.

- Auth fails closed with timing-safe comparison on both edges. E-6 backend/src/server.ts:273: `if (!expected || expected.length < 8) return res.status(503).json({ error: "Backend authentication is not configured." });`; E-151 frontend/app/api/auth/route.ts:13: 

```text
function safeEqual(left: string, right: string) {
  const a = crypto.createHash("sha256").update(left).digest();
  const b = crypto.createHash("sha256").update(right).digest();
  return crypto.timingSafeEqual(a, b);
}
``` Preserve: Keep the min-length fail-closed guard and sha256+timingSafeEqual pattern when adding rate limiting; do not reintroduce any default passcode.

- Server-side gating is layered: proxy session check, relay 401, and a second time-boxed journal lock enforced in the API handler. E-152 frontend/proxy.ts:11: 

```text
// verifies the signed, httpOnly session cookie and never calls a database.
... if (!session.isLoggedIn) { ... return NextResponse.redirect(new URL("/passcode", request.url)); }
```; E-153 frontend/app/api/journal/route.ts:46: `if (entryText.length < 20 || entryText.length > 5000 || !tags || ...) return null; — PRD 5000-char cap now enforced server-side, closing the prior audit finding.`; E-154 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:716: 

```text
if (path[0] === "api" && path[1] === "journal") {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: "Use the private journal route." }, { status: 404,
``` Preserve: The retired legacy POST /api/journal is correctly unreachable from the web app (dedicated encrypted-D1 route handles it; relay blocks the path; backend middleware 410s it) — preserve this triple fence.

- Demo mode is opt-in and fenced away from production data paths. E-155 frontend/app/passcode/page.tsx:18: `localStorage.setItem("jujum-demo-mode", "true"); ... document.cookie = "jujum_demo=1; path=/; max-age=" + 60 * 60 * 24 * 7;`; E-156 frontend/proxy.ts:34: 

```text
if (pathname.startsWith("/api/backend")) {
        return NextResponse.json({ error: "Demo mode" }, { status: 403 });
      }
``` Preserve: Demo fixtures (DemoContext/demoData) never touch Prisma or D1 and demo cookies are rejected at the relay — keep the fence if the sandbox page is repurposed.

- Every manifest script resolves to a real file and all four workspaces carry lockfiles. E-157 tool: `All True: backend build/generate/migrate schema paths, prisma seed ts-node src/seed.ts, expo reset-project.js and frontend npx prisma generate default-schema target all resolve; run_all.bat sanity-checked.` Preserve: Script integrity is complete today — protect it when consolidating the schema copies (F-BLD-9) so no script loses its target.

- All four prior-audit focus findings verified fixed in current HEAD. E-158 frontend/app/explainer/page.tsx:57: 

```text
"Explain entropy and irreversibility",
  "Mohr’s circle for plane stress", — suggestions now GATE-ME scoped (Thermal & Fluid Sciences catalog at :929-959); no CS-topic remnants.
```; E-159 backend/src/gateway.ts:33: `app.post("/api/interview/skip",...) ... upsert({where:{sessionId_questionIndex:{...}}, update:{skipped:true,...}}) — skips persist; page.tsx:25 mints one stable crypto.randomUUID sessionId reused across evaluate(:95) and skip(:63), grouped via @@unique([sessionId, questionIndex]).`; E-160 backend/src/server.ts:2466: 

```text
const aiResponse = await aiChat(sysPrompt, `Review my last 7-day study logs...`);
...
  settings = await (prisma.settings as any).update({ ... data: { weeklyAnalysis, analysisWeekOf ..., analysisStale: false } }); — tracker seven-section AI analysis implemented and cached daily (fixed prior finding 
``` Preserve: Regression-guard these three fixes once F-BLD-5 lands tests — they are exactly the flows a redesign would break first.

- Journal privacy boundary carries two independent layers. E-36 frontend/lib/session.ts:8: 

```text
journalUnlockedUntil?: number;
  journalUnlockFailures?: number;
  journalUnlockBlockedUntil?: number;
```; E-47 backend/src/lib/private-journal-store.ts:74: `const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv("aes-256-gcm", journalEncryptionKey(), iv);` Preserve: Never collapse the journal into the ordinary passcode/session boundary; keep the separate unlock brake, envelope encryption, and ciphertext-only D1 storage.

- Multi-step state changes use real transactions where they matter most. E-199 backend/src/server.ts:860: 

```text
const plan = await prisma.$transaction(async (transaction) => {
      await transaction.routinePlan.deleteMany({ where: { date: targetDate } });
      return transaction.routinePlan.create({
``` Preserve: Keep delete-and-replace plan swaps and credential rotations inside $transaction; extend the same discipline to any new multi-table write instead of sequential awaits.

- Interview evaluation is the model-output validation exemplar. E-238 backend/src/gateway.ts:29: `function interviewIdentity(body: Record<string, unknown>) { const sessionId=typeof body.sessionId === "string"?body.sessionId.trim():""; ... if(!/^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{8,100}$/.test(sessionId)) throw new Error("Invalid interview session.");`; E-239 backend/src/gateway.ts:28: `const dimensions = candidate.dimensions.map((item,index) => { const numeric=Number(item?.value); if (!Number.isFinite(numeric)||numeric<0||numeric>2) throw new Error("Invalid score."); ...` Preserve: Keep strict identity regexes, dimension-count checks, score clamping and rounding on every new AI-consuming endpoint; generalize this validator rather than diluting it.

- Degrade-don't-fail AI integration keeps user data durable ahead of model output. E-240 backend/src/server.ts:224: 

```text
// A temporary D1 outage should reduce journal context, not take the planner or
// coach offline.
```; E-241 backend/src/server.ts:1517: 

```text
const fallbackPlan = await prisma.routinePlan.create({
    data: {
      date: today,
      greeting: "AI is offline, so a simple starter plan is ready.",
``` Preserve: Preserve the pattern: persist user input first, treat model failure as a degraded-but-successful request, and never let an AI outage block writes.

- Input validation discipline is consistent and defensive across every route. E-275 backend/src/server.ts:319: 

```text
if (content.length < 20 || content.length > 5000 || !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(date)) {
      return res.status(400).set("Cache-Control", "no-store").json({ error: ... });
```; E-276 backend/src/server.ts:828: 

```text
if (tasks.length < 1 || tasks.length > 30) {
      return res.status(400).json({ error: "Add between 1 and 30 tasks." });
``` Preserve: Keep per-field type checks, regex date formats, enum whitelists, and numeric clamps on every new endpoint.

- Retry-safe natural keys make every write path idempotent at the storage layer. E-295 prisma/schema.prisma:190: `@@unique([sessionId, questionIndex])`; E-290 prisma/schema.prisma:47: `date DateTime @unique @db.Date` Preserve: Keep unique natural keys plus upsert semantics for any new create-or-update flow; they are why retries never duplicate user data.

- D1 is used as an opaque encrypted-blob store, matching its physics. E-307 cloudflare/journal-store/migrations/0001_create_journal_entries.sql:1: `CREATE TABLE journal_entries (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, entry_date TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL, ciphertext TEXT NOT NULL, ...` Preserve: Never query or filter journal content inside D1; keep ciphertext opaque so the encryption envelope stays meaningful.

- Lockfile discipline is complete across every workspace. E-339 tracked tree: `0 foreign manager locks; four npm lockfiles committed and resolved versions verified current-line (express 4.22.2, next 16.2.9, expo 54, prisma 5.22.0).` Preserve: Keep exactly one package manager and commit every lockfile when adding workspaces; run npm ci once CI exists on main.

- The negative-space record is genuinely reasoned and binding. E-360 PRD.md:30: `Out of Scope: Multi-user accounts, multi-tenancy, compliance, complex evaluation harnesses, paid tiers...`; E-361 PRD.md:17: `The trade-off: The app does not scale, has no monetization, and relies on the creator for backups/uptime.` Preserve: Keep the out-of-scope list authoritative: this audit calibrated severities against it (no RLS demanded, enterprise ceremony skipped), and future contributors should extend the list rather than silently crossing it.

- Error messages follow what-why-next discipline in the user's own register. E-222 backend/src/server.ts:714: 

```text
error: "AI mentor was temporarily unavailable, but your entry was saved safely. Retrying shortly.",
        friendlyMessage: "Aapka entry save ho gaya hai, par mentor abhi offline hai."
``` Preserve: Keep bilingual, outcome-stating messages that distinguish what failed from what was saved; never replace them with generic codes.

- Motion respects user preference at the primitive layer. E-367 frontend/components/MotionComponents.tsx:8: `const shouldReduceMotion = useReducedMotion();` Preserve: Keep every new animation routed through MotionComponents so prefers-reduced-motion stays honored by construction.

- Commit history is disciplined conventional-commit narration. E-431 backend/src/gateway.ts:1: `import crypto from "node:crypto";` Preserve: Keep the conventional-commit convention; it is the raw material for future automated changelogs and releases.

- Secret handling in the deploy manifest is exemplary. E-45 render.yaml:26: 

```text
- key: AI_CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY
        sync: false # Optional key for AI credentials (defaults to SESSION_SECRET if not set)
```; E-454 render.yaml:33: `sync: false # HTTPS URL of the private journal Worker` Preserve: Keep every credential as sync:false runtime injection with explanatory comments; never bake values into the committed manifest.

- AI call telemetry schema anticipates real needs. E-230 prisma/schema.prisma:135: 

```text
model AiCallLog {
  logId String @id @default(uuid())
  surface String
  latencyMs Int
  success Boolean
  errorMessage String?
``` Preserve: Keep per-surface latency/success capture when wiring usage quotas and cost attribution; the table design is already right.

- Build plan anchored modules to specs tightly enough that drift is detectable. E-341 PRD.md:80: `## Section 3 — The Five Modules (Detailed Specs)`; E-498 prisma/schema.prisma:45: `model Journal {` Preserve: Keep plans anchored to named sections so future audits can diff intent against implementation mechanically.

- Tool-native instruction files redirect instead of duplicating. E-514 frontend/CLAUDE.md:1: `@AGENTS.md` Preserve: Keep CLAUDE.md-style files as pure redirects when adding new agent tools so memory has exactly one source per scope.

- Domain vocabulary is perfectly aligned across four artifact layers. E-424 prisma/schema.prisma:210: `model FinanceExpense {`; E-341 PRD.md:80: `## Section 3 — The Five Modules (Detailed Specs)` Preserve: Never introduce synonyms (purchase/spending/outlay) into the finance domain; the one-noun discipline is what keeps both backends greppable.

## Findings

### product

#### F-PRD-1 Success metrics lack thresholds, deadlines, and named sources [Low | Firm | S]

- Where: PRD.md:27
- Evidence: E-340 PRD.md:27: `Daily use: The primary metric of success is consistent daily engagement.`; E-318 PRD.md:26: `₹0 cost: Runs completely on free-tier hosting + a pluggable AI model.`
- Impact: The product's own headline bet (the daily loop is the moat) cannot be verified or refuted because engagement is never operationalized; two of three recorded metrics prove the author can write testable metrics.
- Fix: Rewrite the success block as three measurable lines (e.g. log completion &gt;=5 days/week measured from StudyLog rows monthly; dashboard TTI &lt;2s on free tier; monthly AI cost &lt;= X via AiCallLog counts) and review them in the cron weekly summary.
- Verify the fix: `rg -c measured PRD.md expects >=2 quantified metrics`
- Checks: A-PRD-5
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-127

#### F-PRD-2 Requirements are not priority-ranked and NFR thresholds are incomplete [Low | Firm | S]

- Where: PRD.md:80
- Evidence: E-341 PRD.md:80: `## Section 3 — The Five Modules (Detailed Specs)`; E-170 PRD.md:24: `Fast load times: Desktop dashboard loads under 2 seconds.`
- Impact: Scope cuts under time pressure happen by intuition rather than by rank; only one of ten NFR dimensions has a number, so regressions against intent are undetectable.
- Fix: Tag Section 3 modules Must/Should/Could within the existing caps and give durability, privacy, and AI-quality NFRs one threshold each (backup age, journal encryption coverage, JSON parse success rate from AiCallLog).
- Verify the fix: `rg -c Must PRD.md Section 3 expects >=5 ranked items`
- Checks: A-PRD-6, A-PRD-7
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-127

#### F-PRD-3 PRD completeness gaps: risk/assumption registers, prior art, change control, visual direction, closure plan, traceability [Low | Firm | S]

- Where: see evidence ledger
- Evidence: E-564 PRD: `0 hits for all three sections.`
- Impact: Six product-record sections are missing, weakening future planning and making this audit's requirement mapping partial.
- Fix: Add the six short sections to PRD.md (two registers, three comparables, changelog rule, one visual-direction sentence, retro schedule, requirements table linking Section 3 items to routes/models).
- Verify the fix: `rg -c "## Section 8" PRD.md >=1 plus traceability table present`
- Checks: A-PRD-9, A-PRD-12, A-PRD-15, A-PRD-16, A-PRD-17, A-PRD-21
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-126

### architecture

#### F-ARC-1 A second backend implementation inside the Vercel relay duplicates Express domain logic with divergent semantics [High | Certain | L]

- Where: frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:43
- Evidence: E-161 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:43: `async function handleNativeTrackerRoute(safePath: string, method: string, parsedBody: any, reqUrl: URL) {`; E-20 backend/src/server.ts:2748: 

```text
targetBill = await bill.findFirst({
          where: { OR: [{ id }, { paid: false }] },
          orderBy: { createdAt: "desc" },
        });
```; E-162 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:383: `taskType: "study",`; E-163 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:18: `function getKolkataMonday(date: Date = new Date()): Date {`
- Impact: The same operation behaves differently depending on which platform handles it: fixes applied to one copy silently miss the other, and the drift has already shipped a finance-mutation defect (arbitrary bill payment) that the relay copy does not have. Two platforms hold DATABASE_URL and write the same tables with different invariant enforcement.
- Fix: Choose one writer per table: delete the native tracker/routine/finance handlers from the relay and always forward to Express, or extract shared domain logic into one package consumed by both. Add contract tests asserting identical responses from both surfaces for the same payload.
- Verify the fix: `node -e "const s=require('fs').readFileSync('D:/DOOR/frontend/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts','utf8');console.log((s.match(/handleNative(Tracker|Finance)Route/g)||[]).length)" (expect 2 definitions removed, i.e. 0)`
- Checks: A-ARCH-2, A-ARCH-18, A-ARCH-20, A-ARCH-22, A-ARCH-23, A-CODE-3, A-BUILD-2
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-104

#### F-ARC-2 No backup or restore path exists for the primary Postgres store [High | Firm | M]

- Where: see evidence ledger
- Evidence: E-164 deploy configs, docs, scripts: `0 hits: nothing backs up the Supabase Postgres that holds journals-adjacent data, finances, plans, ratings, and interview history.`; E-165 PRD.md:25: `Data is backed up weekly via JSON export.`
- Impact: A bad deploy, accidental reset endpoint call (/api/tracker/reset and /api/finance/reset wipe tables irreversibly), or Supabase free-tier data loss destroys the user's entire history with no recovery; the PRD's durability promise is unmet.
- Fix: Add a weekly cron job (the existing /cron/tick harness) that exports all tables to JSON and pushes them to Cloudflare R2 or the D1 store, plus document a restore procedure; gate /reset endpoints behind a confirmation token.
- Verify the fix: `rg -n "pg_dump|export.*json|R2|backup" scripts/ .github/workflows/ (expect >=1 hit)`
- Checks: A-ARCH-3, A-ARCH-26, A-PRD-18
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-112

#### F-ARC-3 Vendored LibreChat tree is dead weight with no recorded role [Medium | Firm | S]

- Where: see evidence ledger
- Evidence: E-166 all first-party code and manifests: `0 hits: 164k vendored files referenced by nothing, deployed by nothing, explained nowhere.`
- Impact: Every clone, scan, grep, and future audit pays for 164k third-party files that no code path reaches; the audit fingerprint itself mis-signals product overlays (chatbot/marketplace/logistics matches come from LibreChat text), and a stale vendored chat server is a standing supply-chain liability if anyone ever wires it in.
- Fix: Delete the directory, or move it behind a git submodule pinned to an upstream tag with a documented integration plan; record the decision in one ADR line.
- Verify the fix: `Test-Path D:\DOOR\LibreChat (expect False, or SUBMODULE content with docs/LibreChat-role.md present)`
- Checks: A-ARCH-2
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-126

#### F-ARC-5 server.ts concentrates every domain context in one 2880-line transport file [Medium | Firm | L]

- Where: backend/src/server.ts:undefined
- Evidence: E-562 backend/src/server.ts:1: `import express, { Request, Response, NextFunction } from "express";`
- Impact: Settings, journal, routine, tracker, finance, explainer, cron and JSON-repair logic share one module, so every change risks unrelated behavior and review scope is the whole file.
- Fix: Split into routers per domain (routes/settings.ts, routes/journal.ts, ...) sharing lib helpers; server.ts becomes composition only. Do this alongside GA relay-dedup so there is exactly one copy of each handler.
- Verify the fix: `wc -l backend/src/server.ts expects < 600 after split`
- Checks: A-ARCH-5
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-114

#### F-ARC-6 Tracker study-log backup to D1 is a silent dual write with no reconciliation [Medium | Firm | S]

- Where: backend/src/server.ts:undefined
- Evidence: E-563 backend/src/server.ts:1: 

```text
saveStudyLogToD1({
      id: newStudyLog.id,
      ...
    }).catch(() => {});
```
- Impact: Postgres write succeeds, D1 mirror write fails silently (.catch empty), and reads merge from both stores - counts drift between stores with nothing detecting it.
- Fix: Record d1SyncedAt on each StudyLog row and have /cron/tick reconcile rows older than a day that never mirrored, logging mismatches via the structured logger.
- Verify the fix: `rg -c d1SyncedAt prisma/schema.prisma expects >=1`
- Checks: A-ARCH-8
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-123

#### F-ARC-4 No architecture decision records or diagrams exist for a three-deployable system [Low | Firm | S]

- Where: see evidence ledger
- Evidence: E-167 tracked tree: `0 hits across 193 commits and at least two architecture pivots (journal to D1, plan generation removal).`
- Impact: Pivots are recoverable only from code comments and commit messages; the next maintainer (or the owner in six months) cannot tell why the relay carries a second backend or why plan generation was removed.
- Fix: Add docs/adr/ with three entries: journal-D1 privacy split, relay-native handlers (or their removal per F-ARC-1), routine-plan generation removal; one Mermaid diagram of the three deployables and data stores.
- Verify the fix: `git ls-files docs/adr | Measure-Object -Line (expect >=3)`
- Checks: A-ARCH-4, A-ARCH-14, A-ARCH-15
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-126

### stack

#### F-STK-1 Node runtime is unpinned and CI builds on an EOL version [Medium | Firm | S]

- Where: .github/workflows/vercel-production-check.yml:24
- Evidence: E-309 .github/workflows/vercel-production-check.yml:24: `node-version: 20`; E-310 all manifests: `0 hits: no engines field; no .nvmrc; render.yaml declares no runtime version.`
- Impact: Three environments resolve three different Node versions (CI 20 - past EOL April 2026, local 24, Render default), so build-time behavior differs per surface and security patches for the CI toolchain have stopped arriving.
- Fix: Add engines.node &gt;=22 to every package.json plus a .nvmrc, set node-version: 22 in both workflows, and pin Render's NODE_VERSION env to 22.
- Verify the fix: `rg -c node-version: 22 .github/workflows expects 2 and Test-Path .nvmrc expects true`
- Checks: A-STACK-4, A-STACK-22, A-STACK-24
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-126

#### F-STK-2 Phantom dependencies: backend iron-session unused; root manifest vestigial with version skew [Medium | Certain | S]

- Where: backend/package.json:21
- Evidence: E-311 backend/package.json:21: `"iron-session": "^8.0.1",`; E-312 backend source: `0 imports: sessions live in the frontend package instead.`; E-313 package.json:4: `"@prisma/client": "^5.22.0"`
- Impact: Unused dependencies expand the audit surface, confuse install contexts (which manifest wins depends on invocation directory), and invite wrong-package imports later.
- Fix: Remove iron-session from backend/package.json; delete the root package.json or give it a real role (workspace scripts) with matching prisma versions.
- Verify the fix: `rg -c iron-session backend/package.json expects 0`
- Checks: A-STACK-21, A-BUILD-22
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-126

#### F-STK-3 No stack decision record exists (alternatives, flip points, switching cost) [Low | Firm | S]

- Where: see evidence ledger
- Evidence: E-314 tracked tree: `0 hits: A-STACK-2 pre-flight answers exist only inside PRD prose; losers, flip point, ceiling number, and switching cost are unrecorded.`
- Impact: The bundle works today, but future churn (AI provider changes, Supabase pricing cliffs) will be decided from memory rather than criteria; cheap to fix given the PRD already holds half the content.
- Fix: Add docs/adr/0001-stack-bundle.md capturing pre-flight answers, one rejected alternative per slot, a numeric ceiling (e.g. single user, &lt;100 req/day), and the re-check trigger (any paid tier or second user).
- Verify the fix: `Test-Path docs/adr/0001-stack-bundle.md`
- Checks: A-STACK-5, A-STACK-9, A-STACK-10, A-STACK-11, A-STACK-17, A-STACK-16
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-126

### database

#### F-DB-2 Money columns use Float instead of integer minor units or Decimal [High | Firm | M]

- Where: prisma/schema.prisma:214
- Evidence: E-279 prisma/schema.prisma:214: `amount    Float`; E-280 prisma/schema.prisma:226: `allowance Float    @default(0)`
- Impact: Currency sums accumulate binary floating-point error (0.1+0.2 class) in exactly the budget-vs-spending comparisons the finance module exists for; the check catalog marks floating-point money a hard-cap defect, calibrated here to High because the flows are display-grade single-user records with no settlement or reconciliation against external money.
- Fix: Migrate amount/allowance to Decimal @db.Decimal(12,2) (or integer paise), convert at the client boundary, and round sums with decimal-safe helpers.

- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-119

#### F-SEC-1 AI-generated output is executed with new Function and injected as raw SVG in the renderer [Critical | Certain | M]

- Where: frontend/components/AiMarkdown.tsx:613
- Evidence: E-1 frontend/components/AiMarkdown.tsx:613: 

```text
const fn = new Function("x", `return ${expr}`);
      const val = fn(x);
```; E-2 frontend/components/AiMarkdown.tsx:976: 

```text
if (data.type === "function") {
      ...
      return <FunctionGraphBlock equation={data.equation || "x"} range={data.range} />;
```; E-3 frontend/components/AiMarkdown.tsx:499: `dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: cleanCode }}`; E-4 backend/src/server.ts:2014: `explanationText: JSON.stringify(data)`
- Impact: Any content that steers the explainer model (a crafted topic, OCR text from an uploaded image, or a compromised/malicious free-tier model) can make the browser execute arbitrary JavaScript in the logged-in session via the function-graph block, or inject script-bearing SVG. The payload persists in ConceptExplanation.explanationText and re-executes on every future render of that explanation.
- Fix: Remove the new Function sink: parse equations with a restricted math parser (mathjs evaluate with a narrow node allowlist, or a small shunting-yard evaluator) and render plots from its output. Sanitize every AI-derived HTML/SVG sink with DOMPurify (allowing only svg/path/circle/etc.) before dangerouslySetInnerHTML, and strip event handlers and foreignObject.
- Verify the fix: `rg -n "new Function" frontend/components && rg -c "DOMPurify.sanitize" frontend/components (expect 0 and >=2)`
- Checks: A-SEC-2, A-SEC-9, A-SEC-23, A-SEC-24, A-LLM-4
- Status: open
- Remediation: GA-101

#### F-SEC-10 Bill-pay endpoint pays an arbitrary unpaid bill when the requested bill id does not exist [High | Certain | S]

targetBill = await bill.findFirst({

```; E-21 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:653: `if (!targetBill) return NextResponse.json({ error: "Bill not found." }, { status: 404 });`
- Impact: POST /api/finance/bill/pay with a stale or wrong id marks the newest unpaid bill paid and books an expense for it, silently corrupting finance records instead of returning 404; the same operation behaves differently depending on which of the two backends handles it.
- Fix: Delete the findFirst fallback in backend/src/server.ts and return 404 like the relay implementation; add a regression test asserting unknown ids return 404 and mutate nothing.

#### F-SEC-2 No rate limiting or brute-force throttling exists on any endpoint including passcode verification [High | Firm | S]

- Impact: The internet-facing Render URL accepts unlimited guesses against the single shared APP_PASSCODE that guards every endpoint, and unlimited invocations of paid AI endpoints once any caller holds the passcode. A leaked passcode cannot be contained by lockout because no counter exists.
- Fix: Add express-rate-limit (and a Vercel edge rate limit on the relay): strict window on /api/auth*, per-key windows on AI and export endpoints, and a failure counter with temporary lockout mirroring the journal-unlock brake already present in frontend/lib/session.ts.

#### F-SEC-3 AI credential encryption has no rotation path and falls back to SESSION_SECRET [Medium | Firm | M]

- Where: backend/src/lib/ai/credentials.ts:10
- Evidence: E-7 backend/src/lib/ai/credentials.ts:10: 

```text
const secret = process.env.AI_CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY || process.env.SESSION_SECRET;
  ... return crypto.createHash("sha256").update(secret).digest();
```; E-8 render.yaml:26: `sync: false # Optional key for AI credentials (defaults to SESSION_SECRET if not set)`
- Impact: Rotating SESSION_SECRET (the documented session-signing secret) silently breaks decryption of every stored provider API key, taking AI features down until keys are re-entered; there is no versioned key id to perform a controlled re-encryption rotation after suspected compromise.
- Fix: Add keyVersion column to AiProviderCredential, derive keys via HKDF with a recorded key id, require AI_CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY explicitly (drop the SESSION_SECRET fallback), and add a re-encrypt-on-boot routine that migrates rows from old key versions.

- Impact: Builds do not verify the committed dependency graph (install can drift from package-lock.json), third-party actions float on mutable major tags, and no automated vulnerability gate exists, so a compromised or vulnerable release can ship silently.

- Where: backend/src/server.ts:2864

```text
app.post("/api/finance/reset", async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
  try {

...
    res.json({ success: true, message: "Finance data reset successfully in Supabase." });
```; E-92 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:744: 

```text
const nativeTrackerRes = await handleNativeTrackerRoute(safePath, request.method, parsedBody, request.nextUrl);
    if (nativeTrackerRes) return nativeTrackerRes;

    const nativeFinanceRes = await handleNativeFinanceRoute(...);
    if (nativeFinanceRes) return nativeFinanceRes;
```
- Impact: frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:43-709 natively re-implements tracker/routine/subjects/finance handlers that backend/src/server.ts also serves; the relay tries native-first (comment line 742) and falls back to relaying to Express, so two codebases own one contract and will drift independently (already each carries its own copy of bill-pay expense-upsert logic). Inside server.ts itself, DELETE /api/finance/bill/:id, DELETE /api/finance/bill and POST /api/finance/reset are each registered twice (2802+2842, 2816+2853, 2830+2864); Express keeps the first, so the second trio is dead weight that still references raw `(prisma as any).financeBill` and answers "reset successfully in Supabase" — a stale message from the pre-Prisma era. Dead-code remediation cross-ref F-CODE.
- Fix: Pick one owner per path: keep the Next-native handlers and strip those routes from server.ts (or invert). Delete the second registrations at server.ts:2842-2873 outright and correct the surviving reset message.

#### F-BLD-3 Scaffold `any` stub types and dual model-name casts blind compile-time checking of core entities [Medium | Firm | S]

- Where: backend/src/server.ts:14
- Evidence: E-93 backend/src/server.ts:14: 

```text
// Stub types for initial compilation prior to running 'prisma generate'
type Journal = any;
type ProgressRating = any;
type Subject = any;
type Task = any;
```; E-94 frontend/app/api/backend/&#91;...path&#93;/route.ts:377: `const taskModel = (prisma as any).task || (prisma as any).Task;`
- Impact: server.ts:14-18 declares type Journal = any; ProgressRating = any; Subject = any; Task = any — scaffolding for 'prior to running prisma generate', left in place even though the build script always runs prisma generate first. Every task filter callback typed (t: Task) is compile-time unchecked. The same uncertainty leaks as defensive dual-name casts ((prisma as any).task || (prisma as any).Task, .routinePlan || .RoutinePlan, .financeExpense || .FinanceExpense) throughout server.ts and the Next relay, so a schema rename would fail silently at runtime instead of at tsc.
- Fix: Delete the four stub aliases and import generated types from @prisma/client; settle canonical camelCase delegate names once and remove the || fallback casts (or gate them behind one typed accessor helper).
- Verify the fix: `rg -n "type Journal = any|= any;" backend/src/server.ts returns 0; rg -n "as any\)\.task \|\|" backend/src frontend returns 0; npx tsc --noEmit clean in backend.`

- Status: superseded
- Remediation: none

### launch

## Remediation plan

### Destination

Jujum AI ships one writer per table behind a gated deploy, passes its own CI (typecheck, lint, tests) on every push to main, encrypts and backs up everything the PRD calls durable, treats model output as untrusted end to end, and can prove it with a fresh godaudits audit scoring 80+ at 95% coverage.

Final gate: Final re-audit gate: fresh godaudits pass confirming destination reached (GA-128) [open].
Gate acceptance: Every Critical and High finding resolved or accepted-risk with owner and expiry. Overall score &gt;=80 with coverage &gt;=95 and no active Critical findings. AUDIT.mdx regenerated from validated AUDIT.json at the new commit.
Gate verify: `node D:/DOOR/.agents/skills/godaudits/runtime/godaudits.js validate .godaudits/AUDIT.json --repo . --require-fresh-evidence --write`

### Frontier: 25 takeable now

Closed 0 of 28 remediation tasks; claimed 0; blocked 3. A task is takeable when every task it depends on is closed. Claim a task before starting work so a concurrent session skips it, and re-derive this list with `godaudits wayfind` rather than reading it from a report that may be older than the task state.

## Phase 1: Stop the bleeding

### Wave 1.1

- [ ] GA-101 [W1.1] Remove the new Function evaluator and sanitize every AI-derived HTML/SVG sink
  - Files: frontend/components/AiMarkdown.tsx
  - Depends on: none
  - Reuses: DOMPurify + mathjs restricted evaluator
  - Fixes: AI-generated output is executed with new Function and injected as raw SVG in the renderer (F-SEC-1)
  - Acceptance: FunctionGraphBlock parses equations with a restricted math parser (mathjs or shunting-yard) - no new Function remains.; SVG blocks render through DOMPurify with an svg-only allowlist; no event handlers survive sanitization.; rg finds zero new Function and &gt;=2 DOMPurify.sanitize call sites in frontend/components.
  - Verify: `rg -n "new Function" frontend/components expects 0 && rg -c "DOMPurify.sanitize" frontend/components expects >=2`
  - Checks: A-SEC-2, A-SEC-9, A-SEC-23, A-SEC-24, A-LLM-4

### Wave 1.2

- [ ] GA-102 [W1.2] Mount rate limiting on authentication and AI-spend endpoints
  - Files: backend/src/server.ts, backend/src/gateway.ts, frontend/app/api/auth/route.ts
  - Depends on: none
  - Reuses: express-rate-limit middleware
  - Fixes: No rate limiting or brute-force throttling exists on any endpoint including passcode verification (F-SEC-2)
  - Acceptance: express-rate-limit guards /api/auth*, AI routes and reset endpoints with strict windows.; Repeated passcode failures trigger lockout mirroring the journal brake pattern.; Load test of 50 rapid auth failures receives 429s.
  - Verify: `rg -c rateLimit backend/src/server.ts frontend/app/api/auth/route.ts expects >=2`
  - Checks: A-SEC-6, A-SEC-16

### Wave 1.3

- [ ] GA-103 [W1.3] Return 404 from bill-pay when the requested bill id does not exist
  - Files: backend/src/server.ts
  - Depends on: none
  - Reuses: relay implementation as reference
  - Fixes: Bill-pay endpoint pays an arbitrary unpaid bill when the requested bill id does not exist (F-SEC-10)
  - Acceptance: The findFirst paid:false fallback is deleted; unknown ids return 404 mutating nothing.; Behavior matches the Vercel relay implementation exactly.
  - Verify: `rg -n "paid: false" backend/src/server.ts within /api/finance/bill/pay expects 0`
  - Checks: A-SEC-30, A-SEC-4
